# Divergence ledger

Policy (agreed 2026-08-30): the strip and refactor phases are bug-for-bug
compatible with `main` so the comparison harness stays byte-exact. Every bug found
is recorded here, NOT fixed. Fixes happen in a dedicated phase after the refactor,
one commit per ledger entry, with regenerated goldens and the expected output
change documented in the commit.

Status values: OPEN (present in stripped code), REMOVED-BY-STRIP (the code
containing it was deleted in Phase 1), REMOVED-BY-REFACTOR (deleted by a
Phase 3 refactor stage), FIXED (dedicated fix commit merged).

| # | Location | Bug | Trigger / impact | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `src/ER_binary_func.cpp:78` (`fact()`) | Returns `int`; overflows at n ≥ 13, so `n_choose_r` is garbage for carrier counts k ≥ 13 | Can spuriously flip `IsExactVec` and activate the resampling branch in the efficient-resampling test; only reachable when `--max_MAC_for_ER` is raised above default 4 | FIXED (Phase 3e: n_choose_r computed via multiplicative double recurrence, INT_MAX-clamped — identical values for all k<=12; fact() deleted; accumulate overflow-guarded) |
| 2 | `src/Binary_resampling.cpp:43-58` (`SL_setseed`; was `:49-56` until the 2026-09-14 dead-surface sweep deleted `SL_runif_int` above it) | Ignores its seed argument in the R build (`_STAND_ALONE_` never defined); just calls `GetRNGstate()`. step2 never calls `set.seed`, and under `--nThreads>1` `mc.set.stream` unbinds `.Random.seed` in forked children → clock+pid seeding | If the resampling branch is ever reached (k ≥ 21, or k ≥ 13 via bug #1), step 2 results are nondeterministic | FIXED (Phase 3e: SL_setseed now seeds R RNG stream via set.seed before GetRNGstate, making the resampling branch reproducible; unreached at defaults so goldens unchanged. **Re-checked in Phase 4 C2**, which makes `--nThreads > 1` actually fork: the fork path is deterministic. `R/MarkerShard.R` calls `set.seed(1)` in the parent and forks with `mc.set.seed = FALSE`, so every child inherits one fixed stream rather than a pid/clock-derived one. Gated by golden P1 (the ER path, `--minMAC=1`) reproducing byte-identically at `nThreads` ∈ {1,2,3,4,7,64} and by three repeats at `nThreads=4` being mutually identical. **Amended 2026-09-02 (S2):** the 3e fix re-seeded at EVERY `ComputeExact::Run` entry, which meant every ER marker entered the R API (`Rcpp::Environment` -> `set.seed` -> `GetRNGstate`) whether or not it drew anything -- the hazard that blocks threading the marker loop (F4 in `docs/design/step2-threading-verdict.md`). The call is now conditional on some `m_IsExact[i] != 1`, i.e. on a resampling arm actually running, which is byte-neutral because the arms that draw still re-seed with 1 at entry and the exact recursions consume no randomness at all. Measured by tracing `base::set.seed` with both builds installed side by side: 2 -> 0 calls on P1's configuration (default `max_MAC_for_ER=4`, 2 ER markers, both exact) and 3 -> 1 on P33's (`--max_MAC_for_ER=200`, 3 ER markers of which 1 resamples), with all four runs byte-identical to their goldens) |
| 3 | `src/Main.cpp:2256-2270` | `char* end_prev` declared uninitialised; for `t_genoType=="vcf"` neither assigning branch runs, then `std::remove(end_prev)` executes on it — undefined behavior | Current VCF region path is UB; do not use its outputs as a behavioral reference | REMOVED-BY-STRIP (Strip 5). The sibling `std::remove(end)` calls on plink/bgen parse pointers remain — cstdio remove("") no-ops, preserved bug-for-bug |
| 4 | `R/Util.R:273-316` vs `R/Geno.R:11-20,538-560` | `checkIfSampleIDsExist` and `getSampleIDsFromBGEN` defined verbatim twice; no `Collate:` field so C-locale file order decides — the `Util.R` copies win | Byte-equivalent today, so no behavior change; footgun if either copy is edited. Strip deletes both copies | REMOVED-BY-STRIP (Util.R copies in Strip 1, Geno.R copies with BGEN in Strip 6) |
| 5 | `src/Main.hpp:8-16` | Declares `setAssocTest_GlobalVarsInCPP` with 9 params; definition in `Main.cpp:141-151` has 11. Header is stale and never-matching | No runtime impact (header only included by Main.cpp/LDmat.cpp, Rcpp generates its own decls); documents that Main.hpp cannot be trusted as a contract | FIXED (Phase 3e: declarations aligned to definitions — setAssocTest_GlobalVarsInCPP gains t_MACCutoffforER, fast_logistf_fit gains isfirthconverge, dead case-typo writeOutfile_singleinGroup decl removed) **Appended 2026-09-14 (audit F):** the alignment did not hold -- by 2026-09-14 eight declarations disagreed with their definitions again (every one a function the multi-model or FlexRV work changed), because a mismatched declaration of a function nothing forward-references is a harmless overload the compiler cannot flag. `src/Main.hpp` is deleted; `RcppExports.cpp` carries the checked declaration of every export and the four internal forward declarations live at the top of `src/Main.cpp`. The class is structurally impossible now; the row stays FIXED. |
| 6 | `src/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.cpp` PCG loops (e.g. `:5010-5017`) | PCG stop rule `sum(rVec % rVec) > tolPCG` is an absolute float threshold with no ‖b‖ normalisation; `tolPCG=1e-5` against a float accumulation over N elements is loose | Step-1 numerics only pinned to ~1e-3–1e-2; a last-bit change near threshold shifts iteration count by one. Constrains harness tolerances (not a correctness bug per se) | **DECIDED 2026-09-05: leave, documented as a potential problem.** Options laid out for the owner were leave / normalise the stop rule by the right-hand side norm; the owner chose to leave it and record the hazard. Recorded in `docs/state/next.md`. What a future fix would move, traced 2026-09-05: every sparse-GRM step-1 golden bypasses the PCG loop entirely (`getPCG1ofSigmaAndVector` takes the `gen_spsolve_v4` branch when `isUseSparseSigmaforModelFitting && !isUsePCGwithSparseSigma`, which is every `--useSparseGRMtoFitNULL=TRUE` run at the default `--usePCGwithSparseGRM=FALSE`), so only `binary_denseGRM` and its pgen mirror would move, and no step-2 golden consumes them. [ASSUMPTION: traced through the branch, not instrumented -- the first step of any fix is a perturbed-tolPCG drill to confirm exactly which goldens move.] The hazard for a real analysis is at biobank N with a dense GRM, where the unnormalised residual sum grows with N and a fixed 1e-5 becomes a relatively looser stop. |

Observed as 7 of 70 markers (all the MAC=1 singletons) vanishing from the `--AlleleOrder=ref-first` run, hence P11's 63 data rows. **Not ref-first-specific**: under the default `alt-first`, any marker whose ALT is major takes the same `1-f` subtraction, so this reaches ordinary runs on real data. Every golden uses contiguous default bins and is byte-identical. The characterization test in `test-step2-threads.R` was INVERTED in the same commit -- it previously required the serial gap run to succeed, which is what pinned the bug; it now requires both arms to stop, the threaded one still on the earlier and friendlier R-side `varianceRatioBinsHaveGap` message |

With `length(tempPos) == 1` the selection degrades to a bare numeric, and `get_SKAT_pvalue`'s `BETA_Burden = sum(Score)/(sum(diag(Phi)))` (`R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region_Func.R:381`) then hits the `diag()` scalar branch: `diag(x)` for a length-1 numeric builds a `trunc(x)`-square **identity matrix**, so for a variance below 1 it is 0 x 0 and `sum()` is `0` | **Silent `Inf` in `BETA_Burden_cond` / `SE_Burden_cond` beside a correct p-value**, exit status 0, no warning. Verified against the pinned env's SKAT rather than inferred: `Met_SKAT_Get_Pvalue` **accepts** the bare scalar and returns the right p-value (scalar and 1 x 1 both give p = 0.008711913), so there is NO `try-error` and NO NA rescue — the plausible assumption that the error branch masks this is wrong. `BETA_Burden` is `Inf` on the scalar route vs 4.047619 on the 1 x 1 route, and `SE_Burden = abs(Inf/qnorm(p/2))` is `Inf` too. **The `Inf` is only the small-variance corner.** `diag()` on a length-1 numeric reads it as a DIMENSION, so it *constructs* a `trunc(x)`-square identity and the allocation grows as x^2: `diag(2.5)` is 2 x 2 (denominator 2, a plausible-looking wrong beta), `diag(625)` is 3 Mb, and `diag(4e5)` requests **1.2 Tb** and aborts the run with "vector memory limit of 16.0 Gb reached". The argument here is a *weighted score variance*, so large values are the normal case, not a corner: the bundled quantitative conditional marker golden already carries variances up to 625 on a 1000-sample toy fixture, and `wadjVarSMat_cond = VarMat * weightMat` multiplies that by weights whose beta(1,25) values reach ~25 (so ~625 squared) -- and the variance itself scales with N, so biobank-scale input pushes the requested allocation far past any machine. Net: below 1 the run silently emits `Inf` betas and continues; at realistic magnitudes it **hard-crashes on allocation**, losing the whole job, with an out-of-memory message that names neither the region nor the cause. The only guard on the cell is `length(tempPos) > 0` (`:335`), so a single-marker anno x max_MAF cell (a gene with one LoF variant at `max_MAF=0.0001`) is routine. Path: quantitative trait + `--condition` + K = 1 + `--r.corr=0`. No bundled golden covers it — there is no conditional quantitative region run. Latent sibling, same cause: `:249` `outList$VarMatAdjCond[noNAIndices, noNAIndices]` also lacks `drop = FALSE`; a one-marker region collapses it to a scalar and `:449`'s `[tempPos, tempPos, drop = F]` then raises "incorrect number of dimensions". Found 2026-08-31 during the type-polymorphic-builtin audit; pinned by `tests/testthat/test-unit-region-cond.R` | FIXED, in two layers. The *call sites* gained `drop = FALSE` (`:464`, and the latent sibling `:249`), but the real fix is at the landmine: `get_SKAT_pvalue` now takes the trace as `varSum_Phi = if (is.matrix(Phi)) sum(diag(Phi)) else sum(Phi)`, so `diag()` can never be handed a length-1 numeric by ANY caller, present or future. Fixing only the call sites would have left the quadratic allocation armed. Shape by shape the replacement is byte-identical on every reachable path -- a matrix still gives the trace, a bare vector still gives `sum(Phi)`, which is exactly what `sum(diag(vector))` computed -- so only the scalar case changes, from `0` (hence `Inf`) or a `trunc(x)`-square identity to the value itself, and nothing is allocated. Full golden cycle **byte-identical**, as required: no reachable path passes a scalar today, which is precisely why this was latent rather than observed. Characterization test inverted in the same commit and extended to assert the scalar and 1 x 1 routes now agree on every statistic, that a realistic weighted variance (4e5) is traced rather than allocated, and that matrix/vector inputs are untouched |
| 35 | `R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:2268` | `for(i in 1:(numCate-1))` where `numCate = length(cateVarRatioIndexVec)`, defaulting to `rep(1, length(cateVarRatioMinMACVecExclude))` (`:2266-2267`). With a single MAC category `1:0` is `c(1, 0)`, so the loop runs twice and pass two evaluates `listOfMarkersForVarRatio[[0]] = ...` (`:2270`) | Step 1 **crashes** with "attempt to select less than one element" under `--isCateVarianceRatio=TRUE` with a length-1 `--cateVarRatioMinMACVecExclude`. The option is a free-form comma string (`extdata/step1_fitNULLGLMM.R:301`) and nothing in `R/checkArgs.R` or `R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R` requires length >= 2 (verified). `seq_len(numCate-1)` is empty and correct. Found 2026-08-31 during the type-polymorphic-builtin audit; the `1:0` idiom is pinned by `tests/testthat/test-unit-type-polymorphism.R` | **FIXED (2026-09-05)**: `seq_len(numCate - 1)`. Gated by `tests/testthat/test-step1-guards.R` (standard): a single-category `--cateVarRatioMinMACVecExclude=20.5` with `--isCateVarianceRatio=TRUE` fits and writes one categorical ratio, where HEAD died with "attempt to select less than one element". The `1:0` idiom pin in `test-unit-type-polymorphism.R` pins R, not this code, and stands |
| 36 | `R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:1636` (`Covariate_Transform`), also `R/SAIGE_extractNeff.R:406` | `X1 <- X1[, -idx.na]` omits `drop = FALSE`, so dropping all but one design column yields a bare vector; `N <- nrow(X1)` (`:1650`) is then `NULL` and `X1_new <- X1_Q * sqrt(N)` (`:1653`) dies | Step 1 crashes with "non-numeric argument to mathematical function", a message pointing nowhere near the cause. Reached when `ncol(model.matrix) - length(idx.na) == 1`, i.e. all but one design column collinear; the cheapest route is a **quantitative** trait with a single constant covariate, since the one-covariate short-circuit at `:1024` is `traitType == "binary"`-only and `checkPerfectSep` (`:2120`) is binary-only too. Found 2026-08-31 during the type-polymorphic-builtin audit | **FIXED (2026-09-05)**: `X1[, -idx.na, drop = FALSE]`. Gated by `test-step1-guards.R`: a quantitative fit whose only covariate is a derived constant column drops it with the collinearity warning and continues, where HEAD died in "non-numeric argument to mathematical function" |
| 37 | `R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:962`, same pattern at `R/SAIGE_extractNeff.R:347` | `seq(1, nrow(mmat_nomissing), by = 1)` raises "wrong sign in 'by' argument" when the frame is empty, so the intended `stop("No individuals left in the analysis. Exiting...")` immediately below (`:965-967`) is **dead code that can never fire** | The user gets `seq.default`'s diagnostic instead of the written explanation. Trigger: a `--SampleIDIncludeFile` disjoint from the phenotype file (`:888`); the `--FemaleOnly` / `--MaleOnly` filters at `:913` / `:922` have their own working `nrow == 0` guards. Cosmetic — no computation affected. Found 2026-08-31 during the type-polymorphic-builtin audit | **FIXED (2026-09-05)**, in two places: `seq_len(nrow(...))` at both sites (`R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R`, `R/SAIGE_extractNeff.R`), and the "No individuals left in the analysis" stop moved to where the `--SampleIDIncludeFile` subset is applied -- because with factor covariates a 0-row frame died in `model.matrix`'s `contrasts<-` before ever reaching the original site, so fixing the `seq()` alone would still have hidden the message. Gated by `test-step1-guards.R`: a disjoint include file gets the message, and neither `seq.default`'s nor `contrasts<-`'s |
| 38 | `R/SAIGE_Test_main.R:332-334` | `X_PARregion_mat = rbind(X_PARregion_mat, as.numeric(unlist(strsplit(X_PARregion_list[lxp], split = "-"))))` — the appended row is whatever width `strsplit` returned, and `rbind` recycles silently. A token with no `-` gives length 1, so `rbind(<1x2 matrix>, 5)` becomes the row `c(5, 5)`; if the *first* token is malformed the matrix is 1-wide and the next `rbind` hard-errors on column mismatch instead | **Silently wrong pseudo-autosomal boundaries**, with no warning: the result goes straight to `setAssocTest_GlobalVarsInCPP_X_PARregion_mat()`. There is no validation of `X_PARregion` anywhere — `extdata/step2_SPAtests.R:290` is a free-form character option. Gated on `--is_rewrite_XnonPAR_forMales=TRUE`, so unreached at defaults. Found 2026-08-31 during the type-polymorphic-builtin audit | **FIXED (2026-09-05)**: each `--X_PARregion` token must be `start-end`, numeric, `start <= end`, or the run stops naming the token. Gated by `tests/testthat/test-step2-parregion.R` (standard): a dash-less token, a non-numeric end and a reversed range are each refused by name; the default string is exercised by `test-step2-xnonpar-chrom.R`. The rbind-recycling pin in `test-unit-type-polymorphism.R` pins R and stands |
| 39 | `R/readInGLMM.R:405-414` (`Get_Variance_Ratio`) | `ratioVec_null_noXadj <- as.numeric(ratioVec_null_noXadj)` sits in the `else` branch of `if(length(ratioVec_null_noXadj) > 0)`, so the coercion runs **only when the vector is empty** and can never do anything; the sibling ratios at `:386` and `:396` *are* converted. The `stop("categorical variance Ratio null_noXadj are needed")` guard beside it (`:411`) is unreachable for the same reason — it tests `length == 1` inside the zero-length branch | Logic inversion. The `null_noXadj` ratios are passed downstream with whatever type `fread` inferred for column 1, unlike every other ratio vector: any row that makes that column character (a stray quoted value) sends variance ratios into `setSAIGEobjInCPP` as strings. Adjacent to and compounding LEDGER #13, whose `numeric(0)` pass-through was the mechanism behind #10. Found 2026-08-31 during the type-polymorphic-builtin audit | **FIXED (2026-09-05)**: the coercion and the cardinality guard moved into the populated branch, where the sibling ratios have them. Gated by a block in `tests/testthat/test-unit-varianceratio.R` (fast): categorical `null` rows with a single `null_noXadj` row now stop with "categorical variance Ratio null_noXadj are needed" (the guard was unreachable before), and a well-formed file yields numeric ratios. Every committed ratio file is numeric, so no golden can move |

## Type-polymorphic idiom inventory (audit, 2026-08-31)

Entries #33-#39 came out of a sweep for base-R functions whose *semantics*, not
just their result, change with the type/length/shape of the argument. The
remaining sites are latent — each is unreachable today for a stated reason — but
they are the same hazard and are recorded so a future change does not
un-guard one silently. Verified in the pinned env:

| call | result |
|---|---|
| `diag(m)`, `m` q x q | length-q diagonal (the intended use) |
| `diag(m)`, `m` q x 1 | **first element only**, silently |
| `diag(v)`, `v` length q > 1 | q x q diagonal **matrix** |
| `diag(x)`, `x` length-1 numeric < 1 | **0 x 0 matrix**; `sum()` is `0` |
| `diag(x)`, `x` length-1 numeric >= 1 | `trunc(x)` x `trunc(x)` **identity** |
| `sample(x, n)`, `x` length 1 | samples from `1:x`, **not** from `x` |
| `1:n`, `n == 0` | `c(1, 0)` — the loop runs twice, backwards |

**`sample()` on an index vector that can have length 1.**
`R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:2270` and `:2279` pass `which(MACdata$MACvector > ...)`
straight to `sample()`. A MAC category holding exactly one marker becomes
`sample(47L, 1)` — a uniform draw from `1:47`, i.e. a *different, wrong* marker
silently substituted into the variance-ratio set. Unreachable only because the
`length(...) < numMarkers` check at `:2313` (default 30) aborts the run first;
`--numRandomMarkerforVarianceRatio=1` removes that guard. Same shape at
`R/SAIGE_createSparseGRM.R:89`, where the guard at `:79` allows equality and
`--numRandomMarkerforSparseKin` has no lower bound. `:2261` is harmless **by
accident**: its `MACindex` is `1:nrow(MACdata)`, so the degenerate case is
`sample(1, 1)`, which returns the right answer. `sample.int(length(x))` used as
an index is the idiom that cannot fail.

**`1:n` with a reachable `n == 0`.** Beyond #35 and #37:
`R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:2250` (`seq(1, 0)` silently gives `c(1, 0)`, then a
differing-rows error); `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:1526`, where a non-integral
`ngroup` additionally truncates a partial region without warning;
`R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:1530` `3:length(...)` (guarded by `checkGroupFile`);
`R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:1964-1965` `seq(1:Nval)`, the literal anti-pattern,
doubly wrong at `Nval == 0`; `R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:954` `2:(ncol(mmat)-1)`,
which *reverses* to `c(2, 1)`. Correctly guarded, for contrast:
`R/checkArgs.R:135` and `:144`, `R/Geno.R:137` and `:175`,
`R/SAIGE_Test_main.R:589`, `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:1626`.

**`class(x) == "..."`.** `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region_Func.R:382` was the only one in
the tree. Safe as written (both reachable classes are length 1) but a hard `if`
error on R >= 4.2 the moment a length-2 class appears — `class(matrix(1))` is
`c("matrix", "array")`. Converted to `inherits()` in the audit commit as a
byte-neutral change, matching the idiom already used at `R/MarkerShard.R:218`.

**`if (cond)` where `cond` can be `NA`.** `R/Util.R:103` uses non-short-circuiting
`|` across three comparisons, so a truncated `.index` file (an interrupted run)
makes the whole expression `NA` and raises "missing value where TRUE/FALSE
needed" instead of the intended "modified by user" message. `R/Util.R:224` is
the same shape and is already covered by #23.

**`stringsAsFactors`.** `DESCRIPTION` declares `R (>= 3.5.0)`, where bare
`data.frame()` / `read.table()` still factorise: `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:1555`,
`R/SAIGE_createSparseGRM.R:52`, `R/Util.R:101`, `R/Geno.R:224`. All are rescued
today because their consumers go via labels (`as.matrix.data.frame`, `gsub`,
`write.table`) — latent on R >= 4.0, live on the declared minimum. The pattern
to copy is `suppressWarnings(as.numeric(...))` followed by an `anyNA` check, as
at `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region_Func.R:215` and `R/SAIGE_Test_main.R:21`.

**Safe `diag()` sites, recorded so they are not "fixed".**
`R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region_Func.R:250` `diag(as.matrix(Phi))` — the `as.matrix()`
is the correct defence and is exactly what `:381` lacks (note it would still
truncate silently if `Phi` ever arrived as a length-q *vector*).
`R/Firth.R:334` — `chol()` always returns a matrix (`dim(chol(4))` is `1 1`), so
the log-determinant is right even for a scalar argument.
`R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:2059` — a `Matrix` method.
`R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:264` `VarSVec = diag(outList$VarMat)` — safe
(`VarMat` is an `arma::mat`, so it always carries a `dim`, 1 x 1 included) and
also a **dead assignment**: `VarSVec` is written at `:264-265` and never read
anywhere in the tree.

**Dead code carrying the same hazards** (no callers in `R/`, `src/`, `extdata/`
or `tests/`; all package-exported via `NAMESPACE`'s `exportPattern`, so deleting
them is a public-API change and out of scope for the audit): the whole of
`R/Firth.R` (Firth is implemented in C++ and reached via `--is_Firth_beta`;
`test-unit-firth.R` exercises the Rcpp export, not this file);
`pcg` / `pcgSparse` (`R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:1673`, `:1723`), which contain the
textbook `dA <- diag(A)` at `:1676` and `:1726` plus an unconditional debug
`print("R"); print(Minv)` at `:1681-1682`; `bigGRMPar` / `bigGRMPar_new` /
`refineKinPar`; `ReadGroupFile`; `Util.R::writeOutputFile`;
`checkArgsBool` / `checkArgsNumeric`; and the entire
`regionTestType == "BURDEN"` arm of the R post-processing layer (see #33 for
why it is unreachable).

**Searched for and absent:** no live `sapply` / `apply` / `mapply` (only in the
dead `bigGRMPar*` and commented-out lines), no `which.max` / `which.min`, no
`nchar`, no integer-overflow-prone `sum()`. `vapply` is used correctly with an
explicit `FUN.VALUE`, and every one of the five `merge()` call sites is followed
by an explicit re-`order()` on an index column. The character promotion in the
Cauchy row (`R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:531-569`) is deliberate and documented
in-file as golden-load-bearing — it is not a defect.

## Ledger entries #40 onwards

Same table as the one at the top of this file; it continues here because the
type-polymorphic audit above was inserted between them. **The numbering is one
sequence -- #1-#39 above, #40 onwards below** -- so search this file by number,
not by table. (Before 2026-09-04 these rows had no header and no delimiter row,
so no markdown renderer drew them and the ledger appeared to stop at #39.)

| # | Location | Bug | Trigger / impact | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 40 | `src/SAIGE_test.cpp` `SAIGEClass::getMarkerPval`: the conditional-analysis SPA block (`if(t_isCondition)` ... `if(m_traitType != "quantitative" && stat_c > pow(m_SPA_Cutoff,2))`) vs the marginal SPA block (`if(!t_isER)` ... `if(!isnan(StdStat) && StdStat > m_SPA_Cutoff && m_traitType != "quantitative")`) | The conditional block reads `m1`, `p_iIndexComVecSize`, `NAmu`, `NAsigma`, `tol1` and the four vectors `gNA`/`gNB`/`muNA`/`muNB`, but **only the marginal block assigns them**, under a *different* predicate. The conditional block sits OUTSIDE the `if(!t_isER){...}else{...}` pair that encloses the marginal one, so it runs whether or not the marginal block did. When the marginal branch is skipped, all five doubles are read uninitialised (`double q, qinv, m1, NAmu, NAsigma, tol1, p_iIndexComVecSize;` with no initialiser) and the vectors are read sized-but-uninitialised — undefined behaviour, and `p_iIndexComVecSize` is itself the predicate selecting `SPA_fast` vs `SPA` | Reachable in ordinary conditional analysis of a **binary** trait: any marker whose marginal \|Z\| <= `SPAcutoff` (or which took the ER path, `t_isER == true`) but whose conditional statistic exceeds the cutoff — i.e. exactly the markers conditioning strengthens, which is a normal outcome, not a corner. The garbage `p_iIndexComVecSize` then picks the SPA variant at random, and `m1`/`NAmu`/`NAsigma`/`tol1` feed the saddlepoint directly. Quantitative traits are immune (the block is gated on `m_traitType != "quantitative"`), which is why golden P4 (the only conditional MARKER golden, quantitative) does not reach it; no binary conditional single-variant golden exists. Found 2026-08-31 while implementing Phase 4 C3, by mapping the brace structure to check the plan's caveat about deferring the `gNA`/`gNB`/`muNA`/`muNB` allocations. **Not verified by execution** — [ASSUMPTION: reachability derived from the control-flow structure, not from an observed wrong answer; the read is UB so its effect is not predictable]. C3 defers the four vector allocations out of the per-marker path but deliberately re-sizes them at the conditional use site, so the state that block sees is bit-for-bit what it saw before | FIXED. **The [ASSUMPTION] is now an OBSERVATION, and the trigger was not the one anticipated.** A behaviour-neutral detector (a bool plus one stderr line, no value altered) was built FIRST, purely to ask whether the path executes on real data. It does: **2 of 70 markers on the bundled binary fixture under an ordinary `--condition`**, in every conditioning set tried. But the trigger is the **ER path**, not the "marginal \|Z\| below cutoff" route this entry emphasised: the hits report `isER=1` with `StdStat` 3.94 and 2.63, i.e. **above** the SPA cutoff, so these are markers that would otherwise have been SPA-corrected -- `if(!t_isER)` skips the whole marginal block including its assignments. Fix: a `prepSPAContext()` lambda computes the shared context (`t_gtilde`, `p_iIndexComVecSize`, `m1`, the `p >= 0.5` fills, `NAmu`, `NAsigma`, `tol1`); the conditional block calls it and it is a no-op whenever the marginal block already ran, so that path is untouched and any output change is attributable to the fix alone. C3's four `set_size` calls are removed -- they existed only to reproduce the sized-but-uninitialised state, and since `set_size` does not preserve contents they would silently wipe data on a future size mismatch instead of failing loudly. **Impact measured**: exactly 2 rows change per run, the same 2 the detector flagged. rs44 `p.value_c` 7.81467e-05 -> 0.002349397, a **30x shift in the ANTI-CONSERVATIVE direction** (a spuriously significant conditional association); rs56 0.008495285 -> 0.006259919. `BETA_c` is untouched, as expected -- it does not depend on the SPA context. Every pre-existing golden is byte-identical, which is the FINDING rather than a pass: nothing frozen covered this path, exactly as this entry predicted. **Golden P12 `plink_marker_cond_binary.txt` added** to close that gap; it pins the corrected values and would have differed under the buggy build **Appended 2026-09-14 (dead-surface sweep C4):** the `prepSPAContext()` lambda that `8f3ded00` armed at the top of the conditional block lost its only call in `b60141f9` (the #84 residualisation, which made the conditional block compute its own CGF at the `SPA_ws` call) and stayed in `getMarkerPval` with its 12-line "this is the fix" comment and no caller; deleted, with the history recorded at the declaration site. The fix itself is unchanged and P12/P17 still pin it. |
| 41 | `src/SAIGE_test.cpp:950` (`SAIGEClass::getMarkerPval`, conditional-analysis SPA block) against its own SE at `:929` | The conditional SPA p-value is **printed halved**: `sprintf(pValueBuf_SPA_c, "%.6E", SPApval_c/2)`, while the SE written beside it is built from the UN-halved value (`t_qval_c = quantile(ns, SPApval_c/2)` -> `t_seBeta_c = fabs(t_Beta_c)/t_qval_c`, `:929-934`). The `/2` belongs to the two-sided normal quantile, not to the p-value, and was copied into the print. Regression, dated exactly: the pre-image was `t_pval_c = SPApval_c;` (a double), and `c032c687` (2022-08-17, "format p-values in single-variant assoc tests so very small p-values will not be 0") converted it to a formatted string and carried the `/2` across. `c032c687` is an ancestor of the `v1.1.5` tag commit `f6564bd9` (same day), so **every SAIGE from 1.1.5 onward carries it**; 1.1.4 and earlier do not. The marginal block one screen up prints `t_SPApval` un-halved (`:731`), and the log-p arm of this same conditional block prints `SPApval_c` un-halved (`:959`), so the column is not even self-consistent: a marker whose conditional p underflows to the log-p format is reported correctly while its neighbour is reported at half | **Every reported conditional p-value from the SPA path is 2x anti-conservative.** Trigger: binary trait + `--condition`, any marker whose conditional score statistic clears `SPAcutoff^2` and whose SPA converges — the ordinary case, not a corner. Signature (the geometry that started the hunt -- a user's `p.value` vs `2*pnorm(-abs(BETA/SE))` scatter, which turned out to be the MARGINAL ancestor of this bug, fixed upstream in 2023; see the historical note below): the points split into the identity line plus a **slope-1/2 branch** below the SPA gate, i.e. the printed p equals the ONE-sided `pnorm(-abs(BETA_c/SE_c))`. Measured on the bundled binary fixture (`--condition=rs13,rs79`, 70 markers): **4/70 halved at the default `SPAcutoff=2`, 44/70 at `SPAcutoff=0.5`** — the count tracks the gate, which is the positive control; the ratio is exactly 0.5 or exactly 1.0 with nothing in between. Negative controls: quantitative traits are immune (block gated on `m_traitType != "quantitative"`) — 0/32 in P4/P16; and the MARGINAL `p.value` round-trips exactly under every flag combination tried (default, `SPAcutoff=0.5`, `--is_Firth_beta` with `--pCutoffforFirth=0.1`, `--condition`), so this is specific to the conditional column and not a misreading of the round-trip identity. **Not confined to the printed marker column.** `pval_c` is a `std::string` end to end (`src/Main.cpp:191`), and the `--r.corr=1` BURDEN path parses it straight back with `std::stod` (`src/Main.cpp:2019`) into `CCT_cpp`, so gene-level `Pvalue_Burden_c` **and the region's Cauchy row inherit the halving**: on a BURDEN-only conditional run of the bundled fixture (`--is_fastTest=FALSE`, LEDGER #27), GENE1's Cauchy `Pvalue_Burden_c` is 0.0512759, exactly `CCT` over the halved cells, against 0.0755848 over the corrected ones (at `SPAcutoff=0.5`: 0.0329142 vs 0.0669001). The SKAT-O (`--r.corr=0`) region statistics are computed in R from the score/variance matrices and are NOT affected — all 12 non-Cauchy rows of golden P3 round-trip at ratio exactly 1. Affected committed goldens: `{plink,pgen}_marker_cond_binary` 4/70 rows each, `{plink,pgen}_group_cond_binary.singleAssoc` 1/19, `{plink,pgen}_group_cond_multiweight.singleAssoc` 3/31 (P12/P3/P10 and their PGEN mirrors); P4/P16 (quantitative) are clean. **This entry corrects LEDGER #40's impact figure**: the post-fix rs44 value it quotes, `p.value_c` 0.002349397, is itself a halved print — the un-halved SPA p is 0.004698794, so #40's fix moved rs44 from 7.81467e-05 to 4.70e-03, a **60x** anti-conservative shift, not the 30x recorded there. Found 2026-09-01. Fix when the phase comes: drop the `/2` at `:950` only — `:929` is correct and must not be touched, and the SE is therefore already right, so a regenerated golden should show `p.value_c` doubled on exactly the affected rows with `BETA_c`/`SE_c`/`Tstat_c`/`var_c` byte-identical. MEASURED across all 70 markers on the P30 configuration: the two covariate-collinear markers give 1.7e-16 and 4.2e-16 (machine epsilon, total cancellation) and EVERY other marker gives >= 0.237 -- 14.8 orders of separation, with the threshold ~500x above the cancellation and ~12 orders below the smallest real marker. Under #106's relative rule the solve converges and returns the true, genuinely tiny variance (6.84797e-29 for rs20, ~279 orders of magnitude ABOVE `DBL_MIN`), so P27's two rows printed `BETA=-4.37022e+13 SE=1.20842e+14 p=7.176163E-01` -- the original #44 defect, restored by a fix elsewhere, and WORSE than the first time: a 4.4e13 entry in the `BETA` column raises `rel_err()`'s column-scaled absolute floor by 14 orders and would have masked the other 68 markers of that column in numeric mode. `varianceCollapsed` cannot catch it, because there is no cancellation here: `P2Vec` and `gtilde` are both ~1e-16 componentwise and every term of `var2` has the same sign -- it is small because its INPUT is nothing. MEASURED on the P27 configuration, all 70 markers: rs20 and rs22 give 9.1e-31 and 7.5e-31; EVERY other marker gives >= 0.328. Every duplicate was already present in the pre-kill file and NO marker is lost, so the direction is duplication, never truncation -- benign for a scan you re-run, corrupting for anything that joins or counts rows. **Pre-existing and orthogonal to threading**: the serial restart (`nThreadsMarker=1`, `nThreads=1`) and the in-process restart (`nThreadsMarker=4`) produce BYTE-IDENTICAL 132,695-row files, which is also the parity evidence for S7. That order is the fix: without the flush the recorded count would omit the chunk's own tail and the restart would discard good rows. An index written before this (no byte count) still restarts, with a warning that rows may be duplicated, rather than being refused. (b) That 1 is not inert: BOTH CCT implementations detect `any(p == 1)` and return `min(1, n * min(p))` instead of the Cauchy combination -- a deliberate, documented modification (`R/CCT_modified.R:1`) -- so ONE saturated cell reroutes the WHOLE region's `Cauchy` row to a Bonferroni over its cells while the column keeps its name | **Reproduced by hand (2026-09-04), on the golden that was added to close the quantitative coverage gap.** New golden P37 `plink_group_nofast_quant.txt` has **`Pvalue` exactly 1 on 5 of its 12 cells**, beside `Pvalue_Burden` and `Pvalue_SKAT` of 0.82-0.99 on those same rows. The threshold was then measured directly on the kernel, independently of SAIGE: 400 random (`Score`, `Phi`) pairs through `Met_SKAT_Get_Pvalue` with `r.corr = seq(0, 1, 0.1)` gave `p.value == 1` for **55 of 400**, every one with `min(p.val.each) > 0.777`, and **no** case with `min(p.val.each) > 0.8` returned anything but 1 (largest `min(p.val.each)` among the non-saturated cases: 0.7629). POSITIVE and NEGATIVE control are the two arms of that sweep -- small per-rho p never saturates, large per-rho p always does. Link (b) was then closed arithmetically on P37: GENE2's reported Cauchy is 0.978045700202351 and `min(1, 6 * 0.163007616700392)` is 0.978045700202352, while the true ACAT over the same six cells is 1 -- so the printed value IS the Bonferroni, to the last digit. **The suite already carries the detector** and applies it to one binary golden only: `test-invariants-goldens.R:106-110` asserts `Pvalue` lies between the component tests and within 2x of the smaller, which P37 violates on 5 rows. GATE GAP: every previously committed group golden is BINARY, and none of their cells saturates (P6's largest component p is 0.94 and its Cauchy rows reconcile with ACAT), so nothing could see it. NOT quantitative-specific in mechanism -- only in which bundled fixture reaches the regime | **DECIDED 2026-09-05: leave, documented.** Options laid out for the owner were leave / exclude the exact-1 cells from the Cauchy combination / clamp them to 1 - eps; the owner chose to leave the arithmetic and document it. Documented in the `--r.corr` CLI help and the `SPAGMMATtest` `r.corr` roxygen (the Cauchy row is a Bonferroni whenever any cell's SKAT-O p is exactly 1). Two facts verified against the sources on 2026-09-05: STAAR's CCT returns 1 when any input is 1, and the ACAT package warns and lets tan(-pi/2) run, so the min(1, n*min p) fallback is SAIGE's own edit (`R/CCT_modified.R:1` says so). The ledger's earlier claim that SKAT-O cannot exceed both component tests was too strong: the SKAT-O p is bounded below by the smallest component, not above by the largest, so the defect is the point mass at exactly 1, not the ordering. Characterized by `tests/testthat/test-step2-group-quant.R` (unchanged). Therefore, we used the minimum P value with Bonferroni correction to combine multiple tests when any individual test had P = 1." Both statements in this row stand together -- the code edit IS SAIGE's relative to ACAT and STAAR, AND it is the published method. This STRENGTHENS the owner's 2026-09-05 decision to leave the arithmetic: it is not an undocumented deviation. What remains defensible to criticise is only that the output does not say WHICH rule produced a pooled p-value, which is #127 and not this row |

A BINARY trait cannot reach the floor: tau0 = 1 and W = mu(1-mu) <= 1/4, so tau0/W >= 4. Since #65 landed, `fitNULLGLMM` runs every quantitative solve on the phenotype standardised to unit adjusted variance, so the floor is UNREACHABLE from the command line and from `fitNULLGLMM`; it remains live for a direct caller of `glmmkin.ai_PCG_Rcpp_Quantitative` or `extractVarianceRatio` with an unstandardised small-variance trait, and it is an arbitrary absolute constant in code that #65 had to route around rather than fix. Its floor is DELETED, with no replacement. Sigma's diagonal is a sum of non-negative terms, so the only value it cannot take is a non-positive one, which means the variance components have collapsed -- and `gen_spsolve_v4` ALREADY reports that, throwing `spsolve(): solution not found` when the factorisation fails. The clamp was precisely what stopped that from being reached, by perturbing the diagonal until a matrix that is not the model's Sigma factorised; removing it restores the loud failure the row asked for without adding a new one. `getDiagOfSigma` and `getDiagOfSigma_LOCO` are different: all four of their call sites are `minvVec = 1/getDiagOfSigma (...)`, the Jacobi PRECONDITIONER, which cannot change the solution of a CONVERGED PCG -- but SAIGE's PCG stops on an absolute residual (#6), so where it stopped, and hence the answer, did depend on the clamp. They now clamp only a non-positive or non-finite entry, and to 1 (no preconditioning for that sample), which is neutral and scale-free; a small POSITIVE diagonal is the correct preconditioner for a small-scaled trait and is left alone. A `tau` component below `tol` is set to exactly zero by the AI-REML step (`tauVec.elem(find(tauVec < tol)).zeros()`), so a zero diagonal is REACHABLE and throwing at these two sites would have turned a limping fit into a hard error -- which is why they keep a fallback and only `gen_sp_Sigma` loses its floor. The copy at `:2727` stays untouched: it is inside a comment block. Characterization `tests/testthat/test-step1-sigma-diag-floor.R` (fast tier; no fit needed -- `setupSparseGRM` installs a 6x6 GRM and `gen_sp_Sigma` is exported, so the clamp is reached directly, which is the direct caller this row said no test had) green at HEAD as a pin of the defect, inverted in the same commit, and drilled BOTH ways: 5 failures against the pre-fix scratch install (`harness/work/lib-old`), 0 after the rebuild. It pins two controls that hold either way (a diagonal above the floor, and a binary-shaped W, which cannot reach the floor since tau0 = 1 and W <= 1/4) and two inverted claims: at tau = (2.2e-5, 4.6e-5) -- the bundled quantitative phenotype over 100 -- the diagonal is now the model's own rather than a flat 1e-4, the old clamp having inflated it by a MEASURED 1.47x; and the diagonal is now exactly scale-equivariant, a trait scaled by s giving a diagonal scaled by s^2 (measured 1.33e-4 against the exact 1.00e-4 before). **No golden moved, as predicted BEFORE the run and from the goldens themselves**: the clamp fires only below 1e-4, and the minimum diagonal over all five step-1 golden configurations is 0.216 (quantitative `tau0/W`) and 4.0 (binary, `tau0/W >= 4`), both far above it, so the old and new rules agree bit for bit there. Pre-push gate green in BOTH compare modes |

The "no live reference" claim was re-verified after LEDGER #92 (which had been the only thing naming any of them, in its dead blocks): a grep for each of the 51 basenames over `R/`, `src/`, `tests/`, `harness/`, `extdata/`, `docker/`, `Makefile` and `README.md` now returns only `extdata/input/genotype_100markers_2chr.log`, a generation log. Pre-push gate green in BOTH compare modes after the move |
| 97 | `R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:2662` and `:2675` (the `sparse` rows of `varRatioTable` in `extractVarianceRatio`, multiplied by `varRatioScale`), fed `varRatioScale = 1 / yScale^2` from `:1504` and `:1762` | **The #65 rescale multiplies the `sparse` variance-ratio rows by 1/s^2, but that ratio is scale-free.** On the sparse path both `var1` (`:2578`, G' Sigma^-1 G / AC) and `var2sparseGRM` (`:2588`/`:2598`, g' Sigma^-1 g) go through Sigma^-1, so multiplying y by f multiplies both by 1/f^2 and the ratio is unchanged; only the `null`/`null_noXadj` rows, whose denominators `g'g` (`:2606`) carry no y, scale as 1/f^2. Since #65 the quantitative fit runs on y/s and every ratio row is scaled back by 1/s^2, so the `sparse` rows are wrong by exactly 1/s^2 (s = sd of the covariate-adjusted phenotype); `:2675` even writes `1 * varRatioScale` for the constant fallback. Every quantitative model fitted with `--useSparseGRMforVarRatio=TRUE` since `66a65bb9` carries the error; step 2 consumes the `sparse` row wherever the sparse GRM is used (the region path, the sparse marker `scoreTest`), so those variances are inflated by 1/s^2 and the p-values deflated. Binary traits are untouched (yScale = 1) | **Found 2026-09-05 by the strip-vs-upstream comparison on the N = 50,000 `sim50k` fixture (`harness/sim/upstream_compare/`), predicted as rounding-level and observed at 11-12%:** refitting `y_quant`, `y_quant_pc`, `y_null` at HEAD against the fixture's pre-#65 models gives `sparse 1`/`sparse 2` rows at new/old = 1.11126 / 1.11979 / 1.11169, equal to 1/s^2 computed independently from `lm(y_inv ~ covariates)` residuals (1.11126 / 1.11979 / 1.11169) to five digits, while the `null` rows agree to 1e-6 and theta to 1e-5. So the unscaled standardised-fit ratio equals the phenotype-unit ratio, i.e. the quantity is scale-free, and the applied factor is the entire difference. Ungated: `quant_sparseGRM` is the only quantitative step-1 golden and it has no `sparse` rows (`--useSparseGRMforVarRatio` is TRUE only on the binary `binary_sparseGRM_vr`), and `test-step1-pheno-scale.R` drives the same configuration. The two-way check the #65 handoff reports (y * 100 ratios at 1e-4 x to 5e-8) was on `null` rows only | **FIXED (2026-09-05).** The `sparse` rows of `varRatioTable` (`:2662` in the estimated arm, the constant `1` fallback at `:2675`) are written unscaled; only `null`/`null_noXadj` are multiplied by `varRatioScale`, and the parameter's comment, `R/PhenoScale.R`, CLAUDE.md and README now say so. Test `tests/testthat/test-step1-vr-sparse-scale.R` (standard tier): a quantitative step-1 on the bundled fixture with `--useSparseGRMtoFitNULL=TRUE --useSparseGRMforVarRatio=TRUE`, at y and y * 100 (phenotype file derived at run time by `helper-pheno-scale.R`, shared with the #65 test). Drilled both ways: at unpatched HEAD (scratch library built from `c0688cc9`) the sparse rows scaled by 1e-4 with the null rows (rel_err 0.9998) and the unscaled fit's sparse rows were 1.47 = 1/s^2 (s = 0.82, the adjusted sd on this fixture), 2 failures; after the fix the sparse rows are invariant to 1e-8 and within 0.05 of 1 while the null rows scale by exactly 1e-4, 10 pass. Moved no committed golden, as predicted (no quantitative golden has sparse rows). Any quantitative model fitted with `--useSparseGRMforVarRatio=TRUE` between `66a65bb9` and this commit must be refitted |

**Historical note: the marginal column had #41's exact geometry, and it is fixed.**
Between `d65a709e`/`81c95cb8` (2022-09-08, "deal with p-values = 0", DESCRIPTION at
v1.1.6) and `1f637a58`/`576fe839` (2023-09-09, shipped as v1.3.0 the same day), the
MARGINAL single-variant SE used the ONE-sided quantile:
`t_qval = R::qnorm(t_SPApval, 0, 1, false, ispvallog)` instead of `t_SPApval/2`, and
the Firth arm likewise used `qnorm(pval, ...)`. So `SE = abs(BETA)/z_p` instead of
`abs(BETA)/z_(p/2)` for every SPA-applied (and every Firth) marker -- **SE
overestimated** by `z_(p/2)/z_p`: 28.3% at p = 0.1, 19.2% at 0.05, 6.5% at 1e-3, 2.1% at 1e-8.
Plotting the reported p against `2*pnorm(-abs(BETA/SE))` gives the SAME slope-1/2
branch as #41, gated at the SPA cutoff (`SPAcutoff = 1.6449` puts the identity
line's lower end at p = 0.1 and the branch's upper end at 0.2), **but the correct
value there is the reported p and the SE is the wrong column** -- the exact opposite
of #41, where the SE is right and the print is halved. Affected releases: 1.1.6
through 1.2.0; 1.3.0 onward are clean, and so is this branch (fork HEAD is v1.5.2).
Verified rather than assumed: the marginal `p.value` round-trips at ratio 1 over all
22 committed goldens (6,073 marker rows, PLINK and PGEN) and 11 flag configurations
swept on 2026-09-01 (`SPAcutoff` 2 and 0.5, `--is_Firth_beta` at `pCutoffforFirth`
0.05 and 0.1, `--max_MAC_for_ER=20`, `--is_noadjCov=FALSE`, `--is_fastTest=TRUE`,
`--condition`, quantitative). #41 survived the 2023 fix because that commit corrected
the three *quantile* calls and never looked at the *print* two screens below.

**Reproduced 2026-09-01**, to settle which column is wrong. `1f637a58`'s marginal
line was reverted (one line, `t_SPApval/2` -> `t_SPApval`), rebuilt, and run against
the same 128,868-marker fixture at `--SPAcutoff=1.6449`. Result: `p.value`, `BETA`,
`Tstat` and `var` are **byte-identical between the two builds** -- the defect cannot
reach the p-value, which settles the question: on the branch the reported p is
right and the SE is the wrong column. `SE` differs on exactly the 11,932 SPA rows
that are not ER, and the observed inflation matches the closed form `z_(p/2)/z_p`
to 8.4e-6 relative (median 1.189, max 1.283). Geometry reproduces the reported
figure exactly: the identity branch's lower end and the slope-1/2 branch's upper
end sit at `2*pnorm(-SPAcutoff)` and twice the largest SPA p -- 0.1000 and 0.1995 at
cutoff 1.6449, 0.0455 and 0.1163 at the default cutoff 2, i.e. the elbow tracks the
gate. One subtlety worth knowing when reading such a plot: the **ER path is immune**
(its own `/2` at `:799` was never removed), yet ER markers are flagged
`Is.SPA = true` because the ER branch sets `t_isSPAConverge = true` at `:801` -- 393
of the 12,325 `Is.SPA` rows here are MAC <= 4 ER markers sitting on the diagonal,
so `Is.SPA` does not partition that figure cleanly and is a misnomer on those rows.

Moved no committed golden, as predicted from the value being dead. Characterized by a page-granular allocator, not a repeat count: `harness/guardmalloc.sh` runs the same two-chromosome LOCO fit under `/usr/lib/libgmalloc.dylib` (every allocation on its own page, unmapped page after), reached from `tests/testthat/test-step1-loco-oob.R` in the standard tier, macOS-only. **Drilled both ways:** at the unpatched build (snapshot of the `9347f8e7` install) the clean arm is `status=139` with traceback frame 1 `setStartEndIndex(startIndex, endIndex, j - 1)`, 2 failures; after the fix it exits 0, 4 pass. The script's plant arm -- one deliberate `Get_OneSNP_StdGeno(1999L)`, index 1999 of a 1,666-element `alleleFreqVec`, injected at the same program point -- passes in BOTH, which is the detection-power control and is NOT optional: `Rscript` routes through a SIP-protected binary that strips `DYLD_*`, so the GuardMalloc banner prints from the pre-exec process while the R process that runs the fit allocates from ordinary malloc (measured: an `arma::fvec` sized 1666 read 334 elements past its end and returned zeros, with the banner in the log). Hence `$R_HOME/bin/exec/R` directly, as in `harness/tsan.sh`. A bounds-checked (`ARMA_NO_DEBUG`-less) build does NOT catch this: the access is `alleleFreqVec[i]` and armadillo's `operator[]` is unchecked in every build |
| 103 | `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region_Func.R:384-385` (`get_SKAT_pvalue`), against the burden information `SKAT:::Met_SKAT_Get_Pvalue` uses at its own `r.corr = 1` branch (`a <- as.matrix(sum(Phi))`) and against the collapsed-pseudo-variant beta the `--r.corr=1` path computes at `src/SAIGE_test.cpp:288` | **`BETA_Burden` and `SE_Burden` on the SKAT-O path do not estimate the burden effect: they estimate `kappa_w * b` and `kappa_w * SE(b)`, where `kappa_w = 1'Phi 1 / tr(Phi)` is the LD inflation of the region's weighted score covariance.** Writing `S_w` for the weighted score sum, `A_w = tr(Phi_w)` and `B_w = 1'Phi_w 1`, the score estimator of the burden coefficient `b` is `S_w / B_w` with `SE = 1/sqrt(B_w)`, because the score for `b` is `S_w` and its information is `B_w`. SAIGE instead reports `S_w / A_w`, so `E[BETA_Burden] = b * kappa_w`. NOT an internal inconsistency, and an earlier draft of this row was wrong to call it one: `Var(S_w/A_w) = B_w/A_w^2`, so SAIGE's `SE_Burden = sqrt(B_w)/A_w` is the CORRECT standard error of the statistic it reports, `BETA/SE` reproduces the printed p exactly, and the p-value and Z are unaffected. The gate then found THREE more characterizations of the old behaviour that name no row (`test-step2-file-integrity.R`'s barren-region header test, `test-step2-region-chunkio.R`'s nothing-partial-survives test, and all three `test-unit-region-degenerate.R` tests), inverted in the same commit: the barren region contributes its NA row, a run stopped at the chunk-file failure leaves at most the header, and the degenerate `region_postprocess` returns the one named row (13 columns binary, 11 quantitative, one row whatever K). Related: #76, #113, #126, #127, #128, #59 (the header used to move to the first chunk with rows; it is now written at start and that chunk's `fwrite` rewrites identical bytes)

Verified: `printed == (sum of ALL non-collapsed marker MACs) + (set's pseudo AC)` is exact to better than 0.01 alleles on 345 of P58's 384 sets. THE EARLIER CAVEAT'S RESIDUAL IS EXPLAINED and the refutation in it was wrong: the residual is #109's dosage zeroing acting on the weighted PSEUDO-MARKER (via `imputeGenoAndFlip` just above :3007), not on the non-collapsed markers I first tested. The pseudo-marker's own MAC is the one that must be <= `dosage_zerod_MAC_cutoff`, and that is a PERFECT necessary condition here: of 318 sets whose pseudo MAC exceeds 10, ZERO show a residual; of the 66 at or below 10, 39 do. **THE FIX IS ONE CONDITION** -- `pr.w0(r)` is already in scope at :2610 -- but the same unconditional accumulation is THREE lines wider than this row first said: `MACCase_GroupVec` and `MACControl_GroupVec` (:2606-:2607) and `NumRare_GroupVec` (:2617) are accumulated with no weight condition either, so `MAC_case`, `MAC_control` and `Number_rare` are wrong in the same way and by the same markers. MEASURED: weighting rs4 at 0 leaves `Number_rare` at 3 where deleting rs4 gives 2. All four columns must move together or the output is internally inconsistent **PRIMARY SOURCE, 2026-09-11, and it settles the open sub-decision's direction.** SAIGE-GENE+ (Zhou et al., Nat Genet 2022, doi 10.1038/s41588-022-01178-w, open access as PMC9534766; received 2021-07-11, accepted 2022-07-29), Methods ("Collapsing ultra-rare variants"), verbatim: "Ultra-rare variants with MAC <= 10 are collapsed to a single marker"; "the dosage or genotype for each sample of the collapsed variant is assigned as the maximum raw dosage or genotype value among all ultra-rare variants carried by the sample"; and for user weights, "the dosages or genotypes of the ultra-rare variants are first multiplied by the provided weights and then collapsed". So BOTH the `std::max` collapse (:2664/:2686) and the weight-before-collapse order this branch implements are the published method, not implementation quirks -- which also confirms the custom-set semantics FlexRV inherits. CONSEQUENCE for the open sub-decision: the object actually tested is the max-collapsed pseudo-variant, so a per-set MAC built from it ("test-faithful") matches the published method, while an additive sum of the constituent markers' MACs does not describe what is tested. MEASURED on this fixture, the two differ little: additive would give 56 against the max collapse's 54 on GENE1 (4%) and 42 against 41 on GENE2 (2%), because few samples at N = 1,000 carry two ultra-rare variants; the gap grows with the >= 2-carriers fraction, which is the open F5 measurement in `docs/state/next.md` **SCOPE COMPLETED 2026-09-11, and the defect is REPORTING AND GATING, not arithmetic.** A zero weight behaves differently depending on where the marker sits, and the paper's own weight-before-collapse ordering is why. The reason (ii) is already right is that the weight multiplies the dosage BEFORE the `std::max` collapse, so a zero-weighted ultra-rare marker contributes `max(x, 0)` and is excluded from the pseudo-marker's value for free. In BOTH arms `Pvalue_Burden` is identical to the deletion arm to every printed digit (2.556854E-01 for (ii)), so the burden STATISTIC is already correct and this row is a reporting-and-gating defect -- it becomes statistical only where the wrong MAC crosses a variance-ratio band (20.6%) or the gate (8.3%). FIVE counters are accumulated with no weight condition, at two sites, and all five are in scope: `MAC_GroupVec` (:2604), `MACCase_GroupVec` (:2606), `MACControl_GroupVec` (:2607) and `NumRare_GroupVec` (:2617) for the non-collapsed markers, and `NumUltraRare_GroupVec` (:2690) for the collapsed ones -- the last is a SEPARATE site from the other four and was missed when this row said "four columns". CONSEQUENCE for custom-weighted masks (`weight:NAME` group-file lines): nothing changes at all unless the mask contains an exact 0, and no bundled custom-weight fixture does (`group_new_snpid_multiWeights.txt` and `group_new_snpid_withWeights.txt` have zero of them), which is why no non-FlexRV golden moves **ATTRIBUTION CORRECTED 2026-09-11: this is NOT "upstream's semantics", as this row's WHAT cell and CLAUDE.md both said.** Upstream (365deeff) has NO per-set dimension: `jmr` appears ZERO times in its `src/Main.cpp`, and `MAC_GroupVec`, `NumRare_GroupVec` and `NumUltraRare_GroupVec` are all declared `q_anno_maf` -- per CELL -- and accumulated as `MAC_GroupVec(jm) += MAC`. With one weight set per run the cell's MAC and the set's MAC are the same object there, so the question cannot arise and there is nothing to inherit. The three columns are also mutually CONSISTENT upstream, which is a coherent design and is why no weight condition was ever needed. Phase 5 of THIS branch widened all three to `q_anno_maf_weight` (per set) but left the accumulations unconditional, producing the hybrid measured above: the non-collapsed part of `MAC` and both counters describe the cell, while the collapsed part of `MAC` describes the set. So #118 is a gap in this branch's own multi-weight generalisation, not inherited behaviour -- which removes the bug-for-bug objection entirely, since that rule exists to protect upstream behaviour and there is none here to protect **UNITS DECIDED 2026-09-11 by the owner: option (b), a FULLY UNWEIGHTED per-set MAC.** So `MAC` becomes a genuine allele count -- the sum of the raw MACs of the non-collapsed markers the set kept, plus the UNWEIGHTED MAC of that set's collapsed pseudo-marker -- and a gate threshold of 1 or 5 then literally means alleles, which is what makes #126's decision coherent. CONTEXT that made this cheap: upstream implements BOTH collapse orders the paper describes, `max(genoURMat, GVec)` unweighted at its :1470 for the default beta path and `max(genoURMat, t_weight(i) * GVec)` weighted at :1476 for user-supplied weights, keeping an unweighted twin `genoURMat_noweights` at :1477 alongside. So upstream's own `MAC` is a clean allele count on the DEFAULT path and mixed-unit only on the user-weights path -- the mixed units are not this branch's invention, but FlexRV sets are custom sets and always take the weighted path, which is why every FlexRV row has them. Our twin is `genoURMat_noweights` (:2069/:2687), already maintained and already unweighted, but declared `q_anno_maf` (per CELL). IMPLEMENTATION SHAPE, all line numbers resolved 2026-09-11 and all of it still to be written: widen `genoURMat_noweights` to `q_anno_maf_weight` and gate its accumulation on `w0(r) != 0`; take the pseudo-marker contribution to `MAC_GroupVec` (:3147/:3212) and to `MACCase_`/`MACControl_GroupVec` (:3155-:3157 and its twin site) from that unweighted matrix instead of the weighted `genoURVec`; gate the five non-collapsed accumulations (:2604/:2606/:2607/:2617 and :2690) on the same condition; and make `genoSumcount_noweight` per-set and weight-gated so the gate, the variance-ratio band and ER read it. NOTE the divergence this one DOES carry, unlike the rest of #118: on the user-weights path upstream's pseudo contribution stays weighted, so (b) is a deliberate departure from upstream there and the bug-for-bug rule genuinely applies -- taken knowingly. FREE INTERNAL CHECK that falls out: under (b) `MAC_GroupVec` and `genoSumcount_noweight` become the same quantity computed two ways (`pr.MAC` against `arma::accu` of the non-zero genotypes), so they must reconcile, and any gap between them is a located error rather than a rounding annoyance **IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-11, exactly the shape resolved above.** `genoURMat_noweights` and `genoSumcount_noweight` are both `q_anno_maf_weight` (jmr) now; the five non-collapsed accumulations at :2604-:2618 are gated on `pr.w0(r) != 0` and so is the ultra-rare count at :2690 (on `wUR`, where a zero weight was already excluded from `genoURMat` for free by `max(x, 0*g) == x`); the pseudo-marker's contribution to `MAC_GroupVec` and to `MACCase_`/`MACControl_GroupVec` comes from the unweighted twin at :3147/:3212, computed as `MAC_nw = min(af_nw, 1-af_nw) * 2 * t_n * (1 - missingRate)` from the SAME pre-`imputeGenoAndFlip` vector the weighted `MAC` is. DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED: the UR pseudo-marker's OWN single-variant test context at :3085 still reads the weighted `MAC`, because that row tests the weighted pseudo-marker; that is why every `*.singleAssoc.txt` is byte-identical. GOLDEN MOVEMENT, predicted per file in `docs/state/prediction-118-126-127.md` before the gate and confirmed: on a custom-weight golden with no zero weight (P8/P9/P10/P21/P22/P24/P34/P37) ONLY `MAC`, `MAC_case` and `MAC_control` move -- no p-value, BETA, SE, `Number_rare` or `Number_ultra_rare` -- and they move to the values the PLAIN golden of the same cell already printed, because `group_new_snpid_multiWeights.txt` and `group_new_snpid_withWeights.txt` have `var`/`anno` lines byte-identical to `group_new_snpid.txt`; so the CADD row and the REVEL row of a cell now print the same MAC (`GENE1 lof 0.001`: 38.4 and 3.34 both become 5, matching `plink_group_nofast.txt`). The FlexRV goldens P58/P59/P60/P61 move further, because they are the only bundled inputs with a weight of exactly 0 **MEASURED AT N = 50,000 ON 2026-09-11, and it is BIGGER than this row's bundled-fixture figures suggest.** sim50k `y_null`, 210 genes, 192 FlexRV sets, 40,196 cell rows, control `35654e19` against HEAD: `MAC` moves on 96.4% of rows and `Pvalue_Burden` on 8.8%, median relative move 0.8% but MAX 20%. The band ratio cannot explain 20% -- this model's two `null` ratios are 1.0892 and 1.0951, 0.54% apart, worth ~0.3% in SE. The second route is `ctx.useSparseGRM = (MAC > m_cateVarRatioMinMACVecExclude.back())`, 20.5 at the default: a set whose real MAC is small now takes the sparse-GRM score test that the CELL's MAC used to let it skip, which is a different variance ESTIMATOR and not a different band. Counted on the two output files, rows with MAC <= 20.5 went 2,722 -> 4,287 (+57%). So on real data #118 is a statistical change to ~9% of burden p-values, not the reporting-and-gating fix the bundled fixture makes it look like -- and the 0.29% SE figure recorded above is the BAND alone and must not be quoted as the size of the row. Impact on tests at this relatedness is small (variance ratios of the 0.97 and 1.31 models differ by well under 1%; the collapsed-vs-fixed comparison in #121 moved per-gene log10 p by ~0.005), but the estimate is wrong wherever the cycle is entered and a dense-relatedness cohort would feel it. A bracket needs the score to be a function of tau2 alone, but the score at a tau2 depends on the inner (alpha, b) state that `Get_Coef` converges only to `tol.coef`, so a sign from an early iteration is stale: with the bundled fit's own trace (0.3357, 0.3725, 0.3362, 0.3275) a bracket from the first three signs has lo = 0.3357 and would bisect the fourth step instead of accepting 0.3275, changing a golden; on the 50k trace it would build [0.10, 0.26] around a fixed point of 0.97. Zero is an iterate: a fit that lands on 0 evaluates the score there next and stops only if it is non-positive (printed `tau2 = 0 is the optimum ...`; the inner state is handed to the post-loop refit, a first attempt crashed there and the new test caught it). MEASURED: both readings are inert on every committed golden -- `plink_group_burden.txt` and `plink_group_multiweight_burden.txt` are byte-identical at gate 0, 1 and 5 (no bundled cell falls below MAC 5), the smallest cell MAC in any of the 33 committed group goldens is 3.34, and the smallest per-set MAC over P58's 384 sets is exactly 1.000 with none below. IMPLEMENTATION GATE, and it is deliberately two runs: at gate 1, no row may appear or disappear anywhere and the only movement is #118's MAC column and variance-ratio band, predicted per file beforehand (gate 1 does NOT mean nothing moves -- #118's per-set MAC still moves 174 MAC values and 79 bands); at gate 5, the burden path stays byte-identical and the SKAT-O path loses exactly the cells below MAC 5, which is 2 rows in each of 5 goldens, named in advance. The ONE cross-path invariant that may be asserted is that the SET of refused cells agrees between `--r.corr=1` and `--r.corr=0` on the same data and gate, compared on cell identity (Region, Group, max_MAF, Weight) and NEVER on p-values -- see the 2.76x above; no tolerance spans that and a test that tries is measuring the estimator difference **SUB-QUESTION RESOLVED 2026-09-11 by the owner: ONE flag, default 1, applied to BOTH paths.** The reason is stronger than the one first recorded here: the SKAT-O path does not merely contain a burden component, it REPORTS all three (`Pvalue_Burden`, `Pvalue_SKAT`, and `Pvalue` for SKAT-O itself), so a gate on burden testing that skips the path printing `Pvalue_Burden` is incoherent. Burden therefore becomes more permissive on real data (refusing below MAC 1 where it refuses below 5 today); no bundled fixture can show that, since every committed burden golden is byte-identical at gate 0, 1 and 5 **PRIMARY SOURCE, 2026-09-11: this row's "divergence from upstream" framing is too strong and is corrected here.** SAIGE-GENE+ (Zhou et al., Nat Genet 2022, doi 10.1038/s41588-022-01178-w, open access as PMC9534766; received 2021-07-11, accepted 2022-07-29) describes NO minimum-MAC filter for a group at all -- the only "minimum" in its Methods is the minimum-p-value fallback of the Cauchy combination -- so there is no published method to diverge FROM; the flag is an undocumented implementation knob, and the value 5 and the burden-only restriction have no published rationale. TIMELINE, which explains the silence: the paper was received 2021-07-11, `minGroupMAC_in_BurdenTest` first appears upstream on 2022-03-12 (v0.99.3, `git log -S` in the `SAIGE-main` worktree at 365deeff), and the paper was accepted 2022-07-29 -- so the filter was added DURING peer review and the Methods were never updated for it. It is therefore not outdated or superseded, it is undescribed. It also probably never touched a published result: the paper's analyses are SKAT-O throughout (nine SKAT-O tests per gene), where the gate is inert by construction, and burden appears only in the REGENIE2 comparison. NOT CHECKED: the online documentation and release notes, where a rationale could still live; absence of a published reason is not absence of a reason **IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-11.** The default moved 5 -> 1 in `extdata/step2_SPAtests.R`, `R/SAIGE_Test_main.R` and `man/SPAGMMATtest.Rd`; the SKAT-O half is enforced in `region_postprocess`'s cell loop on `outList$MAC_GroupVec[jmr]` (the per-set unweighted MAC of #118, which is the burden path's `genoSumcount_noweight` computed the other way), a refused cell emits no row and clears the carry-forward flag exactly as `c.tested == false` does on the burden path, and the refusal is counted for #127's sidecar. **Gate 1 turned out to be inert BY CONSTRUCTION once #118 landed**: a cell that survives `indexNonZeroVec_arma.n_elem > 0` has at least one allele, so an unweighted MAC of at least 1. THE GATE-5 PREDICTION IN THIS ROW IS VOID and is corrected here: it named the `REVEL lof 0.001` rows at MAC 3.34 and 3.55 (2 rows in 7 goldens, not 5) as the cells the SKAT-O path would lose, but those are weighted-unit values and #118 makes them 5, which gate 5 admits. A test that wants the gate must now NAME a value -- `test-step2-region-threads.R`'s stale-record fixture was rewritten to pass `--minGroupMAC_in_BurdenTest=5` rather than inherit it **NULL CALIBRATION CHECKED 2026-09-12** on sim50k (N = 50,000; `docs/state/prediction-126-null-calibration.md` with the outcome appended, raw numbers `harness/sim/calib126/results-2026-09-12.txt`, prediction written before any run). The cells admitted only at gate 1 are calibrated on the quantitative null in every MAC stratum -- windowed burden, 1,186 primary cells, ECDF(0.05) 0.059 / 0.057 / 0.054 / 0.050 at MAC 1 / 2 / 3 / 4, pooled 0.056 [0.043, 0.070], the MAC 5-20 reference 0.059; the same cells on the SKAT-O path (`Pvalue` and `Pvalue_Burden`, identical there because a sub-5 cell is one pseudo-marker) and the 192-set FlexRV arm agree -- and conservative on the binary null exactly as discreteness predicts (pooled 0.023 [0.015, 0.033]; strata 0.020-0.025 against the 1 - 0.99^m expectation 0.010-0.039; never above alpha; the case-carrier cells sit at p 0.01-0.04 and the rest at 0.85-0.95). Gate 5 removes exactly the MAC 1-4 rows and nothing else moves: 0 shared cell rows differ across 22 run pairs, and every printed MAC equals the allele count computed from the genotypes with the ultra-rare collapse taken as a per-sample max (0 mismatches over 1,875 + 5,051 + 4,128 cells; the raw allele sum is wrong by the co-carrier count, 387 cells on the gene file). Positive control, 63 causal genes: detections equal at both gates on the burden and FlexRV arms (14 and 11 of 63) and 16 -> 15 on the SKAT-O grid, the one loss (GENE0183, 9% inside the threshold) being CCT dilution by three copies of one MAC-2 cell at p 4.5e-4, ratio 1.21 against the (n+k)/n bound 1.5. The check found #128 (a raised gate kills SKAT-O runs) and re-observed #116. |

(a) rs4's CADD weight set to 0: the Cauchy row equals the REVEL row in Pvalue, Pvalue_Burden and Pvalue_SKAT (0.67865699121867), against 0.531915 for the untouched control -- and GENE2, with no zero weight, combines normally, which is the negative control. (b) the `GENET var rs28 rs32` cell (MAC 2) under `--r.corr=1`: the cell row is absent and the Cauchy row reads `Pvalue_Burden = 0.5`. (c) counted, not observed, from the per-set MAC computation in #118. Originally: NOT fixed. The candidate fix is one mechanism for all three routes -- have the pooled row (or a sidecar in the shape of `.skatFailures.txt`, #57) record asked / tested / refused, and refuse to print a pooled p-value when nothing was tested. The precedent for the counting line already exists in this tree: a threaded region run prints `SKAT-O cells: N asked for, M evaluated, K served`. This is a SCHEMA-shaped change if the counts go in the row, so it would move every group golden, and a sidecar is the cheaper option that moves none; that choice is the owner's. It should be settled alongside #118 and #126 rather than after them, because both of those shrink the denominator further and would otherwise each add a new silent route **REFOCUSED 2026-09-11 by the primary source.** The Bonferroni fallback that route (a) runs into is prescribed by SAIGE-GENE+ (Zhou et al., Nat Genet 2022, doi 10.1038/s41588-022-01178-w, open access as PMC9534766; received 2021-07-11, accepted 2022-07-29) verbatim (see #86), so this row must NOT be read as claiming the fallback arithmetic is wrong. The defect is DISCLOSURE, which makes the row smaller and better defined: a reader of the output cannot tell which rule produced a pooled p-value, nor how many cells or sets went into it, nor that any were dropped or refused. The fix is correspondingly narrower -- record the rule used and the asked/tested/refused counts, and refuse to print a pooled p-value when nothing was tested -- and the sidecar option (moving no golden) becomes the obvious one, since nothing about the arithmetic needs to change **IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-11** as the owner's option (4): a sidecar, `<SAIGEOutputFile>.pooledTests.txt`, in the shape of `.skatFailures.txt` (#57) and for the same reason -- no result file moves a byte. `R/PooledRow.R` holds the recorder, the rule namer and the summary; the SKAT-O path records from `region_postprocess` (where the cells are tested) and the BURDEN path from `src/Main.cpp` (where its pooled row is written), and `getPooledRecordsInCPP()` merges the two into one table. Columns: `Region`, `Model`, `Path`, `Rows`, `Pvalues_combined`, `Cells_refused_minGroupMAC`, `Rule`, `Pvalue_pooled`, where `Rule` is the branch CCT actually took -- `Cauchy`, `min-p Bonferroni` (#86's prescribed fallback, reached when some cell p is exactly 1), `zero`, or `none`. Written ONLY when a region has something to disclose, so a clean run produces no file and the one-line summary always prints. Route (b) is closed in the arithmetic: `CCT_cpp` of no inputs returns 0.5, and the BURDEN emitter now writes `NA` instead, with the fastTest write decision explicitly preserved (`!anyCombined \|\| cctpval >= 0.1`) so that only the printed number changes. TWO THINGS A READER MUST KNOW: on the BURDEN path a cell whose test returns "NA" writes no row at all, so `Rows == Pvalues_combined` there by construction and route (a) is visible only on the SKAT-O path; and `region_postprocess` is driven TWICE on the parallel SKAT-O path, so `skatoCollect` passes `record_pooled = FALSE` -- only the SERVE drive, which holds real p-values, may record |

| 130 | `src/Main.cpp:3376` (`cs.nonzeroDense(genoURVec.memptr(), indexForNonZero)` -- the ultra-rare pseudo-marker's nonzero list, taken BEFORE the flip), `src/Main.cpp:3408` (`imputeGenoAndFlip(genoURVec, ...)`, which REPLACES genoURVec with `2 - genoURVec` when altFreq > 0.5), consumed at `src/Main.cpp:3456-3490` (the six emission lines `cs.sumAt(cs.slotOf[indexForNonZero(k)], jmr) += genoURVec(indexForNonZero(k))`). Upstream `main` `src/Main.cpp:1553 / 1569 / 1574-1581`, the same three statements in the same order | **A FLIPPED ultra-rare pseudo-marker enters the BURDEN sum as `2 - G` over the UN-flipped carriers, with every non-carrier's 2 dropped.** `indexForNonZero` is computed on the pre-flip vector and `imputeGenoAndFlip` then overwrites the vector in place, so the emission loops read the POST-flip values at the PRE-flip carrier positions: a carrier with G = 2 contributes 0, a carrier with G = 1 contributes 1, and the non-carriers that the flip just made 2 contribute nothing at all. The single test of the same pseudo-marker uses the POST-flip list instead (`indexNonZeroVec`, which imputeGenoAndFlip returns and which the URCell record stores at `src/Main.cpp:3648`), so the two consumers of one collapsed pseudo-marker test DIFFERENT vectors. On a `--r.corr=1` run the column so built is what `Pvalue_Burden`, `BETA_Burden` and `SE_Burden` are computed from, and it is neither of the two coherent readings: the flipped vector and the un-flipped one differ by an affine map that the covariate-adjusted score test is invariant to, so BOTH would give the pseudo-marker's own p, and the emitted column gives a third number. The sign goes with it -- the flip's `Beta * (1 - 2*flip)` correction is applied on the single path and not here, so a one-marker cell reports BETA -0.05205 singly and +2.34563 in the region row. That is upstream's behaviour, verified by reading `main` at 365deeff rather than inferred: it calls `imputeGenoAndFlip` on every marker of the region path (`src/Main.cpp:1269`), recomputes `MAF = std::min(altFreq, 1 - altFreq)` immediately after, weights with `w0 = pdf(beta_dist, MAF)` on that folded value, and has NO guard, warning or flag anywhere for an ALT-major variant in a group test (grepped, C++ and R). So this is not a divergence to be repaired but a published convention to be kept until there is a reason to leave it. The reason it is recorded rather than closed: at an ALT-major `lof` site the burden counts the REFERENCE allele, which the annotation does not describe, while the MAF filter and the beta weight treat the site as rare -- so a cell labelled "rare LoF" can be driven by a rare allele that carries no annotation. The framing that makes the choice tractable is that the real question is BINNING, not coding: the rare allele at such a site has no annotation, so it belongs in no annotation-based bin, and the candidate answers are (a) leave it [CHOSEN], (b) admit and count on AF(ALT), which puts a common-allele burden through machinery calibrated on the rarer allele's MAC, or (c) warn or refuse on such sites, which removes them rather than re-coding them and disturbs no statistics. **REVISIT TRIGGER, and it is one number:** the fraction of ANNOTATED sites in a real callset whose ALT allele is major. If that fraction is negligible -- which it will be wherever a pipeline already filters on MAF or AAF before SAIGE -- (c) is a warning that never fires and (a) costs nothing. If it is a few percent, the choice moves real results and (c) deserves a fixture and goldens. The convention as a whole is written down in CLAUDE.md ("Allele convention") so it is not re-derived. No characterization test, deliberately: what one would pin is the choice, and the choice is now recorded here. Related: #130 (the collapsed pseudo-marker is NOT a variant and is the one deliberate exception to the fold), #133 (the folded/un-folded inconsistency this convention exposes in the MAC columns), #118, #28/#29 (the marker path's exact flip invariance) **PARKED HERE 2026-09-13, from the #133 work, and it is a QUESTION rather than a defect.** A BETA weight set applies a function of MAF -- `dbeta(MAF, a, b)` over every output row -- to the collapsed pseudo-marker's CARRIER frequency, which is not an allele frequency of anything: for an unweighted collapse it is `sum(max(g)) / 2N`, the share of samples carrying at least one of the set's ultra-rare variants. The consequence is BLOCK DOWN-WEIGHTING, measured at N = 1,000 with the default `beta(1,25)`: a single singleton (MAF 5e-4) gets weight 24.70, while a pseudo-marker collapsing 2 / 5 / 10 / 20 / 50 such singletons with disjoint carriers gets 24.41 / 23.54 / 22.17 / 19.64 / **13.62** -- i.e. **the collapsed block is weighted LESS than any one of its own constituents**, monotonically in how many it contains. **This is UPSTREAM SAIGE-GENE+'s rule, inherited with the beta path.** It was NOT introduced or changed by #133, which only made the pseudo-marker's reported MAC and MAF raw rather than folded; the beta path read a carrier frequency before that fix and reads one after. **The sim study's null AND power arms were run under it** (`harness/sim/flexrv_study`, `docs/state/handoff-2026-09-13-flexrv-sweep-rerun.md`), so nothing in those results is invalidated by it -- they simply describe this weighting. It becomes a DECISION only if someone wants the beta path to match the paper's weight-before-collapse arithmetic, which is the option argued AGAINST on blast-radius grounds: it would move every beta-set group golden rather than the handful this class of fix has touched so far. Recorded so the next reader sees the question stated rather than rediscovering it from the numbers. Related: #17 (why a custom set's weight is baked into the collapse instead), #132 (the same carrier-vs-allele confusion in the ACAT-V combination weight, fixed), #133 (which made the reported count honest without touching this).
