It is that **the collapsed PSEUDO-MARKER's MAC and MAF were folded through `std::min` while its VECTOR and its halves were not**. Every REAL marker was already consistent: `imputeGenoAndFlip` flips the VECTOR and rewrites `altFreq`/`altCounts`, and `src/Main.cpp:2746-2747` derives `MAF`/`MAC` from the post-flip values, so vector, counts and halves describe one allele. Since LEDGER #130 the pseudo-marker's vector is never folded and its halves are `cs.paritySumRanked` over that unfolded vector -- but `:3444-3446` still folded its FREQUENCY, so the number disagreed with the vector it described. One marker, not the cell. **BOTH CANDIDATE FIXES IN THE STATUS WERE MIS-LOCATED; do not re-open them.** Candidate A (fold the case/control halves per stratum) is INCOHERENT on a mixed cell -- measured on `plink_group_urmixed_burden`, a per-stratum cell-level fold returns NEGATIVE values (-1064, -997) on four of six cells, because the cell MAC is a sum of PER-MARKER folded counts while the halves are raw cell-level sums, and no cell-level fold reconciles them. Candidate B (report unfolded AC in `MAC`) was said to move `MAC` in every group golden; it does not -- only three goldens have any row where the identity fails. **The fix**: at `src/Main.cpp:3444-3494`, `MAC` and `MAC_nw` become the RAW allele counts directly (`altCounts * (1 - missingRate)`) instead of being derived from a folded frequency. **`MAF` and `MAF_nw` STAY FOLDED, deliberately** -- a first attempt made them raw too, on the false premise that `MAFVec` is a reported quantity. It is not: nothing writes it to any output, and its only consumers are the two `dbeta()` calls in `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R` (`:324`, `:430`). The pseudo-marker's only reported frequency is `AF_Allele2` in the single-variant file, already raw. Making MAF raw therefore changed the WEIGHTING, not the reporting, and the measurement is worth keeping: every p-value stayed byte-identical and `BETA/SE` kept its ratio (-0.210239), but `BETA_Burden` went from -0.860935 to **-1.01393e+13**, the coefficient per unit of a burden weighted to nothing, because `dbeta(0.778,1,25)` = 5.1e-15 against `dbeta(0.222,1,25)` = 0.0605. That number is now the evidence on #131 that the beta-vs-carrier-frequency question is a MODELLING decision rather than a reporting one. The shipped fix touches the two counts only. **Gated, prediction written BEFORE the gate** (`docs/state/prediction-133.md`): exactly THREE goldens moved -- `plink_group_urflip_burden`, `plink_group_urflip_skato`, `plink_group_urmixed_burden` -- the only runs whose pseudo-marker exceeds alt frequency 0.5 (5 rows each). Every other group golden is byte-identical because `min()` is the identity below 0.5; the next highest pseudo-marker anywhere is 0.1548 and most sit at 0.0270, measured across every committed group `.singleAssoc.txt`. No `.singleAssoc.txt` moved at all: its `AF_Allele2` was always the raw `altFreq`, only `MAF`/`MAC` were folded. **Direction confirmed**: `MAC` ROSE to equal `MAC_case + MAC_control` on every affected row (444 -> 1556, 708 -> 1292, 460 -> 1540, 2452 -> 3064, 1687 -> 1915, 2343 -> 2997) and stayed put on the rows whose pseudo-marker is below 0.5. **Two second-order effects, predicted not discovered**: #134's variance-ratio band and ER predicate read `MAC_nw`, which changes for exactly these cells; and the beta-set pseudo-marker weight moves in the SKAT-O drill, `dbeta(0.222,1,25)` = 0.0605 -> `dbeta(0.778,1,25)` = 5.1e-15, a factor of 8.5e-14 that effectively drops the pseudo-marker from that run's weighting. That is the honest reading of a pseudo-marker carrying 78% of the alleles, but it is large and must not be misread as a regression. **Characterization WIDENED, which is what the fix to the test is**: `MAC == MAC_case + MAC_control` is now asserted over EVERY group golden carrying a `MAC_case` column, not just `plink_group_fastTest.txt` -- the narrow assertion is precisely why this was never caught. Red at HEAD on exactly 15 rows (5 in each of the three drills) and green everywhere else, verified BEFORE the goldens were re-baselined. The test also asserts the three drills still CONTAIN the regime (a pseudo-marker above 0.5), so it cannot go vacuous. `CLAUDE.md`'s allele-convention rule 5 gains the reporting half, so the reporting rule and the weighting rule now say the same thing. **A related question is PARKED ON #131, not fixed here**: a beta set applies `dbeta(MAF, a, b)` to the pseudo-marker's CARRIER frequency, which block-down-weights a collapsed set below any of its own constituents. That is upstream SAIGE-GENE+'s rule, inherited with the beta path, unchanged by this fix. Characterized in `tests/testthat/test-step2-region-flip.R` (`fl_expect_flip`): the case/control sum equals the UNFOLDED AC on every cell, the two agree below AF 0.5 and differ above it, so a fix cannot land unnoticed; the narrow assertion in `test-invariants-goldens.R` now says why it holds there. Candidate fixes: fold the case/control halves the same way `MAC` is folded (`MAC_case = min(AC_case, 2 N_case - AC_case)`, which is a per-stratum fold and can disagree in direction with the cell's own fold when the two strata differ -- name that before choosing it); or report the UNFOLDED AC in `MAC` too and let `MAF` carry the folding, which is the smaller change but moves `MAC` in every group golden. Either needs the min-group-MAC gate (#126) re-examined, since it reads the folded `MAC_GroupVec`. Related: #118 (the MAC is an allele count, and per set), #131 (the same fold, in the weighting and admission), #126 (the gate that reads this MAC) |

(5) `isCateVarianceRatio`'s roxygen promises "If TRUE then LOCO=FALSE" and NOTHING enforces it: every `LOCO <- FALSE` in the fitter is conditioned on something else (`useSparseGRMtoFitNULL`, `isLowMemLOCO`), so the two options combine freely and the user's LOCO choice stands. (6) the `--IsOverwriteVarianceRatioFile` refusal opens with "WARNING", says the new ratios "will be output to" the file, and then `stop()`s the run -- it is a fatal error dressed as a warning that describes work it is not going to do. A spec whose MAF transforms are ALL degenerate rungs is refused by name. The ladder's VALUES are untouched (the `flexrv-plan.md` decision stands; the design doc carries the new paragraph). `N = NULL` keeps the full ladder for the unit tests and the name builders. **The evidence the decision rested on** (`docs/state/handoff-2026-09-13-night-135-139-142.md`; `harness/sim/flexrv_study/pool_rules.R`): at the paper's N (~424k, bound ~847) no default rung is degenerate and the shipped rule IS the paper's statistic, so this rule equals it bit for bit there; at N = 50,000 three rungs go (144 sets), on the bundled fixture five (112); measured over the sim50k study every candidate rule left the null ECDF inside the binomial interval and the power identical or within one detection, with the per-gene `p_FlexRV` moving up to 5x in either direction -- a question of definition, settled for the rule that makes `p_FlexRV` independent of N through duplication and skips 25-42 % of the work. Value-identical dedup (drops legitimately identical tests for data reasons) and a weighted CCT (a statistic the paper does not define) were rejected. **Goldens moved as predicted from their own rows before the gate**: P58, P59 and P61 (max MAF 0.01, N = 1,000) go from 193 to 113 rows per gene, GENE1's pooled row 0.139077 -> 0.0956982 and GENE2's 0.907609 -> 0.93903 on P58 (the CCT over the 112 surviving rows, recomputed from the old golden's printed digits before the run), P61 stays byte-identical to P58, P60 (no `mac` rung in its spec) is byte-identical to its committed bytes; every regeneration reproduced twice. The characterization the request specified inverts cleanly and is `test-step2-flexrv-rungs.R`: the warning names exactly the rungs at or above `2 * N * maxMAF`, the emitted set count is 192 minus (those rungs x 16 score transforms) = 112, none of the dropped names appears, and with `--flexRV_maxMAF` raised past 500 / (2 * N) no rung is dropped, no warning prints and all 192 sets are emitted; `test-step2-flexrv.R`'s row-for-row duplicate test became the absence assertion, `test-invariants-goldens.R` builds its expected names with N. Roxygen, `man/`, the CLI help, CLAUDE.md's FlexRV trap and `docs/design/flexrv-plan.md` state the rule; `docs/flag-surprises.md` F5 carries the pointer. Related: #127 (the sidecar's `Rows` now counts the surviving sets), #138, #86, #118 Three notes from the adversarial scout of the change: the WHERE cell's ingest-gate line is `src/Main.cpp:2783` at HEAD (2751 when the row was written); a run written before this change (192 rows per region) and RESUMED after it would interleave 192- and 112-row regions, since the restart guard checks only the Weight column's presence (recorded, not fixed -- FlexRV is days old); and the external weight-line route in the tests deliberately keeps all 192 lines, so the 80 rows the internal run no longer composes are asserted equal to their `unw` row as the second computation that they were duplicates. The sim study's `flex192` arm would test 144 sets on any re-run; its committed results are correct about their date. The comparison is `>=` and the kernel is `MAC <= t`, so a rung EQUAL to the bound is degenerate too: MAC500 goes at N = 250,000 exactly, not only below it (the WHAT cell's "below" is the row's original wording). Reviewed adversarially before the commit (three read-only lenses over the diff: bound arithmetic incl. a brute-force search for a double-rounding case that drops a non-degenerate rung -- none in N <= 2e6 x 21 max MAFs; test vacuity; help-string twins), which found the flexRV_score help describing the drop as rungs that "cannot select anything" -- the all-zero case, which is deliberately KEPT -- and a self-comparison hole in the external-route loop; both fixed in this commit, plus a `docdrift.py` rule for the falsified fixture sentence. The regenerated `.index` files carry the #140 byte counts the committed ones predated (excluded from the byte gate). |

| 156 | `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region_Func.R:411-419` (`get_SKAT_pvalue`): calls `SKAT:::Met_SKAT_Get_Pvalue(Score, Phi, r.corr, method, Score.Resampling)` with five arguments, omitting the sixth | **The exact cubic eigendecomposition is taken for every SKAT-O cell, although the installed SKAT ships an O(q^2 k) alternative and exposes it.** `Met_SKAT_Get_Pvalue`'s signature ends `isFast = FALSE` and forwards it to `Get_Davies_PVal(..., isFast = isFast)`; left at the default, every cell goes through `Get_Lambda`, whose `eigen(K, symmetric = TRUE, only.values = TRUE)` is O(q^3) in the kernel dimension q (eigenvalues-only already, so the cheap constant, and the small-eigenvalue truncation happens AFTER the decomposition, saving nothing). `Get_Lambda_Approx` instead takes the top `k = min(floor(q/10), 100)` eigenvalues by `RSpectra::eigs_sym` and moment-matches the remaining spectrum to one repeated eigenvalue whose Satterthwaite df is fixed by the trace and `sum(K*K)`. NOT a bug in SAIGE's arithmetic: the exact path is correct, and the fast path is APPROXIMATE, so wiring it up means a new option, never a default | `SKAT_davies` is the single largest line item in a SKAT-O group run -- **44.9% of the run** at `sim50k` (N = 50,000, 210 genes, `harness/bench/NOTES.md:575`, Rprof, two reps within 0.3 pp, with a `--r.corr=1` negative control correctly reporting 0.0%). Cost is ~cubic in q, so it concentrates in the widest cells: on All of Us `afr` chr20 (N = 70,272, 537 genes, 6 annotation masks x 3 max-MAF bins) the all-variants union mask `pLoF:damaging_missense:other_missense:synonymous` carries the large majority of the cubic term. **The first q figures computed in the AoU session (notebook only; not written to any doc, and NOT in `docs/state/handoff-2026-09-16-aou-upstream-compare.md`, which predates them) used `Number_rare + Number_ultra_rare` and are WRONG**: the ultra-rare markers collapse into ONE pseudo-marker column (`src/Main.cpp`, the `max()` accumulation into `urAt(sl, jmr)`; `Number_ultra_rare` counts what went IN), so the kernel dimension is `Number_rare + 1` and every share must be recomputed on that before this row's prize is sized. **MEASURED 2026-09-16, and the prize is small: 1.15-1.57x on the SKAT-O call, not the ~24x the flop count predicted, and it does NOT grow with q** (`Met_SKAT_Get_Pvalue(Score, Phi, r.corr = 0, method = "optimal.adj")` on synthetic rare-variant kernels, N = 3,000, q in 50..2,000, 2-5 reps: 1.53x / 1.20x / 1.21x / 1.15x / 1.57x / 1.32x, with p agreeing to 3-4 significant figures). The flop ratio q^3 vs q^2 k was wrong by more than an order of magnitude because it ignores BLAS LEVEL: dense symmetric `eigen()` is blocked BLAS3, while `RSpectra::eigs_sym` is Lanczos, i.e. ~k repeated BLAS2 matrix-vector products at O(q^2) each, memory-bandwidth bound. At ~1.5x on a term that is 44.9% of the run, the whole-run ceiling is ~1.2x -- in exchange for an approximate p-value. **The eigensolve ALONE is SLOWER on the fast path below q ~ 3,000** (same box, dense `K = crossprod(G)`: at q = 500/1000/2000/4000 `eigen` takes 0.025/0.071/0.485/3.351 s against `eigs_sym`'s 0.039/0.191/0.585/1.892 s, i.e. ratios 0.64x / 0.37x / 0.83x / 1.77x), so whatever small gain the full call shows below that comes from the Davies quadrature having k+1 rather than q eigenvalues to sum over, NOT from the decomposition. The asymptotics also do not say what they appear to: with `k = min(floor(q/10), 100)` the fast path is Theta(q^2 k), which for q < 1,000 is Theta(q^3/10) -- still CUBIC, a constant-factor change only -- and becomes Theta(q^2) only once k saturates at 100. **AND THE SCOPE IS NOW MEASURED ON REAL EXOME DATA, WHICH CLOSES THIS ROW: q NEVER GETS LARGE.** On All of Us v8 exome chr20, cohort `afr`, in-sample N = 70,272, 537 genes, 9,515 cells, the kernel dimension `q_eff = Number_rare + 1` (the ultra-rare markers having been collapsed into ONE pseudo-marker column) has **median 3, p90 21, p99 62, max 482, and NOT ONE cell above 1,000**. The crossover measured above sits between q = 2,000 and 4,000, and at q = 500 the fast eigensolve is 0.64x, i.e. SLOWER -- so on this workload `isFast` would slow down every cell, not some of them. A median 3x3 kernel is also why the cubic term was never the cost driver in the first place (q^3 = 27). Where it could still matter and this row would have to be reopened: a much larger N (more markers clear MAC > 10 for a fixed max MAF, so `Number_rare` grows roughly with N), `--MACCutoff_to_CollapseUltraRare=0` (then q is every variant in the cell), or gene-SET/pathway aggregation where a region spans many genes. The win is concentrated in exactly the cells the AoU grid decision may remove, so the two interact: shrink the grid and this prize shrinks with it | **OPEN, raised 2026-09-16.** Not yet attempted. Gate before it can be considered: a simulation study comparing both paths on the SAME cells, reporting the p-value distributions and -- because gene tests are read in the tail -- the agreement at small p specifically, plus the realised wall-clock ratio as a function of q. Owner has a machine with the disk space for it. Two things that are already known and must not be re-derived: the fast path only pays for large q (at q = 20, `k = floor(q/10)` is 2 and Lanczos overhead dominates), and turning it on would move EVERY region golden, so it is an option with its own goldens, not a swap. Do not enable it by default under any measured speed-up  **STUDY RUN AND FULLY RECONCILED 2026-09-19. The fast path exists, but NOT on the path SAIGE uses, and its guard is q >= 2000.** `SKAT:::Get_PValue` is the only place `Get_Lambda_Approx` is reachable, behind `if (!isFast \|\| p1 < 2000) exact else approx` -- so the approximation needs BOTH the flag AND a kernel dimension of at least 2,000. The multi-rho SKAT-O path does not go through it: `SKAT_META_Optimal_Get_Pvalue` calls `Get_Lambda(Phi_rho, isFast = isFast)`, and SKAT 2.2.5's `Get_Lambda` body runs `eigen(K, symmetric = TRUE, only.values = TRUE)` unconditionally and NEVER READS the argument. Drilled at q = 2,200, above the guard: with `r.corr = 0` (the plain path) isFast=TRUE calls `Get_Lambda_Approx` once, moves p from 1.128e-217 to 9.981e-234 and takes 0.769 s against 0.918 s (1.19x); with the 8-rho grid -- what the region path actually runs -- it calls it ZERO times, returns a BIT-IDENTICAL p, and takes 10.688 s against 10.686 s (1.000x). **This RECONCILES the PROVENANCE cell rather than contradicting it**: that measurement used `r.corr = 0`, which despite `method = \"optimal.adj\"` short-circuits to the plain path, so it measured a real speedup on a path SAIGE never takes. An earlier version of this note claimed the flag was simply dead -- WRONG, and corrected here. Confirming evidence from the study this row asked for: 10,293 real multi-rho cells from the finished-build guard sweep, both arms on the SAME cells, ratio EXACTLY 1.000 in every q and p band, max abs log10 ratio 0, wall clock 475.50 s vs 473.34 s = 1.005x, 0 errors -- as it must be, since every one of those cells is below the 2,000 guard (max q 134). SKAT is unchanged since the original measurement (2.2.5, built 2025-09-14, pinned r-skat-2.2.5 in pixi.lock), so version drift is refuted. RECOMMENDATION (owner's call, not taken here): close as not reachable. Wiring it to the SKAT-O path means REPLACING `Get_Lambda`, a third-party internal, not setting an option; the guard would then still exclude every real cell (All of Us q_eff median 3, max 482, none above 1,000 -- per this row's own WHAT cell); and on the one path where it does fire it moved p by 17 orders of magnitude in the deep tail, which is a reason against it even where it works **UPSTREAM CHECKED 2026-09-19, and it changes this row's FUTURE but not its answer.** CRAN's current SKAT IS what is pinned -- 2.2.5, published 2023-01-15, `r-skat-2.2.5` in `pixi.lock` -- so there is no CRAN upgrade to take. The development repo `leelabsg/SKAT` (master at Version 2.3.2 when checked) has MOVED the `isFast` branch INTO `Get_Lambda`: `Get_Lambda(K, isFast = FALSE, FastCutoff = 2000)` now dispatches to `Get_Lambda_Org` or `Get_Lambda_Approx` itself, and `FastCutoff` is threaded from `Met_SKAT_Get_Pvalue` through `SKAT_META_Optimal` to `SKAT_META_Optimal_Get_Pvalue` (`R/SKAT_Meta_Optimal.R:52`). So on 2.3.2 the approximation WOULD be reachable on the multi-rho SKAT-O path, which is exactly what it is not on 2.2.5 -- this row would go from 'structurally unreachable' to 'reachable but deliberately guarded'. It would still never fire at defaults: `FastCutoff` is 2000 and the real q on this row is median 3, max 482. **And an upgrade buys NO Davies speed: `src/qfc.cpp` is BYTE-IDENTICAL between 2.2.5 and 2.3.2, as is `R/SKAT_CompQuadForm.R`** (diffed from both sources). Eight other `src/` files differ -- `Binary_Permu_SKAT`, `Binary_resampling`, `DArray.h`, `NPsort.h`, `bed_reader`, `interface_to_R.h`, `kernel_func`, `mwo_reader` -- and SAIGE references none of `bed_reader`, `kernel_func` or `mwo_reader` at all, since it hands SKAT its own Score/Phi and reads genotypes itself. There is no NEWS file in the dev repo, so this is read from the diff, not from a changelog. Upgrading is a gated change regardless: it would move every region golden, and the pin is deliberate|

Predicted golden movement: NONE -- a stall never terminates, so no `converged = TRUE` golden contains one, and every committed binary golden leaves 0 at iteration 1 (trace 0.3357 ...); the one behaviour change outside the band is a `Dtau < 4.08e-4` fit exiting at iteration 1 instead of 2, which no golden or test exercises. Characterization test: `h_s1_t0.02` from the recipe above (20 iterations, `converged = FALSE`, refused with gates on) inverted to 1 iteration and `converged = TRUE` with theta (1, 0); keep `c_K03_t3` (negative score) green with its message.
